- **Title / institution / date:** â€œHatching Year â€“ Northern Saw-whet Owlsâ€ â€” Project Owlnet, live page, accessed 2026-10-30T01:52:44+08:00.

## Reused evidence, taxonomy and corrected inference

- **FUR-CORNELL-CAM2010:** Texas nest-camera synthesis reused for nest-box context, darker/buffer/more spotted female tendency, paler male, and the long 35â€“65-day flight-feather development window.
- **FUR-MIK2013-82:** printed pp.82â€“84 / PDF pp.84â€“86 previously read; reused for whitish facial disc with orange-brown rim, dark eyes, creamy bill, pale feathered legs/toes and *pratincola* adult colour direction.
- **Current / historical name:** current project list uses *Tyto furcata* (subspecies *T. f. pratincola*); older literature frequently uses *Tyto alba pratincola*. This v4 does not merge the American taxon with project #01 European *T. alba*.
- **Corrected design inference:** v1â€“v3 chose a late, nearly adult-feathered juvenile. V4 selects the directly pictured P20 locomotor boundary so stage-specific down, wing length and support can be inspected. â€œCan stand/walkâ€ does not mean branch-perching or flight.
- **Unknown:** individual sex, exact day, wild-vs-hand-raised timing shift, precise feather-tract sequence, toe geometry and an individual adult pairâ€™s ratio. Science remains false. The user-uploaded Mikkola file was unavailable in this runtime; only the already indexed page notes are reused.

## Full image-generation prompt

Create a landscape 3:2 natural-history concept plate in the established warm painterly-realistic owl style, titled exactly â€œAMERICAN BARN OWLâ€ with italic subtitle â€œTyto furcata pratincolaâ€. Show exactly three birds left-to-right with exact labels â€œADULT MALEâ€, â€œ~20-DAY JUVENILEâ€, â€œADULT FEMALEâ€. Setting: a clean but natural wooden Texas ranch nest-box/loft opening with broad flat floor, sparse pellets/dry grass and open grassland light beyond; no woven nest, eggs, prey, humans, UI, border or extra birds.

Adults: heart-shaped whitish facial discs with warm orange-brown rim, dark eyes, creamy horn bills, gold-grey mottled upperparts. Male very pale/white below with sparse spots; female modestly fuller/darker/buffer and more spotted, but height ranges overlap and the display difference must remain subtle. Pale feathered legs/toes, complete natural talons, secure two-foot support.

Center juvenile: specifically around post-hatching day 20, when *T. f. pratincola* can just stand/walk but is nowhere near flight. It is smaller, low/upright with both feet fully supported on the flat box floor; body overwhelmingly wrapped in loose white natal down, rounded head without a finished adult heart-shaped disc, open dark eyes and small pale bill. Wings are only short side stubs with a few emerging dark brown quills/short juvenile feathers, not long folded adult wings; tail absent or hidden. Do not show branch perching, wing-spread, flight or adult-regular spot rows. Exactly three birds; all text legible and exact.

## Reused evidence and corrected inference

- **BEN-RAM2009:** full primary PDF re-read, especially printed pp.519â€“521 and plate sequence. It documents whitish natal down and closed eyes at 0â€“5 days, pin sheaths/eyes open at 7 days, onset of prejuvenile moult around 14â€“15 days, and full juvenile plumage by about 35 days, persisting beyond 60 days. Its Puducherry sample is preliminary; the paper warns against broad generalisation.
- **BEN-MIK2013-274:** adult boundary reused: orange-yellow eyes, greenish-horn/slate-black bill, brown ear tufts/dark crown, tawny-brown black-mottled upperparts, small upper-breast streaks and fine/faint lower crossbars. Female wing/tail measurements are longer, but sexed masses are unavailable; do not state â€œfemale heavier.â€
- **Corrected design inference:** v1/v2 used the well-supported neonatal stage. v3 deliberately changes to the separately documented c.35-day full-juvenile-plumage stage. â€œFull juvenile plumageâ€ is not â€œadult plumageâ€ and not proof of flight ability; growth variables are decoupled.
- **Unknown:** exact individual hatch date, sex, subspecies/local morph, colour calibration, and a quantified first-flight threshold. Science remains false. The user-uploaded Heimo Mikkola file was unavailable in this runtime; no page citation is invented.

## Full image-generation prompt

Create a landscape 3:2 natural-history concept plate in the repositoryâ€™s established warm, painterly-realistic wildlife style, titled â€œINDIAN EAGLE-OWLâ€ with italic subtitle â€œBubo bengalensisâ€. Show exactly three birds, left-to-right, with exact bottom labels â€œADULT MALEâ€, â€œ~35-DAY JUVENILEâ€, â€œADULT FEMALEâ€. Place them in a rocky semi-desert ravine at warm dawn/dusk, with mineral ledges, sparse dry scrub and natural contact shadows; no frame, UI, scale bars, prey, eggs or extra birds.

Adults: near-equal visible body height; do not make the female categorically heavier. The female may have a subtly longer closed wing/tail only. Both have orange-yellow irises, a greenish-horn to slate-black bill, prominent brown ear tufts, dark crown, white brows and blackish facial ruff; tawny-brown upperparts mottled black; small narrow dark upper-breast streaks and fine faint lower-belly crossbars, not a solid dark Cape-Eagle-Owl breastplate. Feathered reddish-yellow tarsi/toes with only cautiously suggested greyer bare outer joints and dark claws. Both feet naturally supported.

Centre juvenile: explicitly approximately 35 days, at the documented full juvenile plumage stage, not a neonatal chick and not a miniature adult. Rounded juvenile proportions, softer buff-grey/tawny juvenile contour plumage, folded developing wings with complete juvenile cover but no exaggerated adult-hard regular barring, shorter tail, residual softer texture around head/shoulders, orange-yellow opening-to-bright eyes and dark small bill. Resting/crouched low on a broad mineral ledge with both feet/support visible; wings closed, no airborne pose, no claim of sustained flight. Keep clear age contrast while avoiding unsupported exact feather-count or sex claims.

- **Stable source ID:** `CAP-OGADA-KIBUTHU2012`
- **Title / authors / year:** â€œBreeding Ecology of Mackinderâ€™s Eagle-Owls (*Bubo capensis mackinderi*) in Farmlands of Central Kenyaâ€; Darcy L. Ogada & Paul M. Kibuthu; 2012.
- **DOI / original URL:** https://doi.org/10.3356/JRR-11-90.1 ; author-uploaded full text read at https://www.researchgate.net/publication/257412835_Breeding_Ecology_of_Mackinder%27s_Eagle-Owls_Bubo_capensis_mackinderi_In_Farmlands_of_Central_Kenya

- **Actually read:** full-text Methods pp.328â€“329 and Results pp.329â€“330, plus abstract.

# 2026-10-29 Â· #62 *Ketupa sumatrana* v3 â€” late dependent juvenile boundary

## BEOW-SGBIRDS-RAPTOR-2021

- **Title / institution / date:** â€œSingapore Raptor Report, Late Spring Migration, Aprilâ€“June 2021â€; Singapore Bird Group / Nature Society (Singapore); 11 July 2021.
- **Original URL:** https://singaporebirdgroup.wordpress.com/2021/07/11/singapore-raptor-report-late-spring-migration-april-june-2021/
- **Accessed:** 2026-10-29 (UTC+08:00).
- **Source type:** regional field-report aggregation with credited photographs; directly opened and read.
- **Species / stage:** *Ketupa sumatrana* under historical *Bubo sumatranus*; recently fledged juvenile.
- **Evidence:** HTML lines 44â€“47 report a fledgling photographed at Rifle Range Link on 4 April, with two adults at the same locality the previous day. This independently supports branch-capable fledgling presence and nearby adults, not a nest-bound wing-bud stage.
- **Limitations:** no measured age, feather lengths, sex or continuous development series; the report does not alone establish the late 2018 plumage boundary.
- **Decision:** use only as a stage/behavior cross-check; no flight action or sex inference is added.

## Reused source IDs and corrected inference

- **BEOW-SGBIRDS-BREED-2018:** directly re-read. Lines 10â€“15 give a dated 7â€“17 August sequence; the juvenile was estimated 8â€“10 months, almost full-sized, had nearly completed moult to adult-like feathers, retained whitish head/ear tufts, attempted hunting and flew with a snake while an adult remained nearby.
- **BEOW-BESG-JUV-2010:** directly re-read. Lines 18â€“23 describe a snowy-white juvenile already adult-sized and capable of good flight but unable to feed independently. This is a different dependent stage and must not be blended with the 2018 late-moult bird.
- **BEOW-SGBIRDS-2026:** adult ID boundary and broad juvenile white-with-dark-bars summary.
- **Corrected design inference:** v2's small, white, extensively barred begging juvenile blends the 2010 white adult-sized dependent stage with the 2018 almost-adult late-moult stage. v3 deliberately selects the dated 2018 boundary: almost adult size, adult-like body/wing feathers, whitish head/ear tufts only, closed bill and secure branch support.
- **Unknown:** exact hatch date, continuous moult sequence, quantified sex ratio, juvenile sex and subspecies variation. Science remains false. Heimo Mikkola upload unavailable; no page citation invented.

## Full image-edit prompt

Edit the supplied #62 Barred Eagle-Owl (Ketupa sumatrana) v2 concept in place. Preserve photographic realism, humid Southeast Asian forest/quarry-edge habitat, the title BARRED EAGLE-OWL, italic Ketupa sumatrana, exactly three owls and the three existing bottom labels. Preserve both adults exactly in pose, near-equal visible scale, pitch-black eyes, large yellow bills, very long horizontal outward ear tufts, dark brown-and-buff barred upperparts, blackish-and-whitish finely barred underparts and natural branch support. Make a bounded juvenile-only science revision to the documented August 2018 late dependent stage: enlarge the center juvenile to almost adult size, perched securely on the same branch; most body and wing plumage already adult-like and fully feathered, with mature-looking dark/buff barred folded wings and tail; retain conspicuously whitish head and ear tufts as the remaining juvenile character. Closed bill, alert but non-flight pose. Do not depict a small fluffy nestling, pure natal down, begging gape, shortened wing buds or hidden tail. Do not invent a fixed female-larger rule or sex the juvenile. No explanatory callouts, extra text, prey, eggs, extra birds, UI or border.

Lines 23â€“24 describe the nest in a bird's-nest fern on a rain tree and nearby adult sentry perches.

Design inference: preserve adult and historical v1 evidence, remove any implied sex-size exaggeration, use the explicit older feathered juvenile stage, and show a non-nesting lowland Halmahera branch because nesting biology is not established by the read sources. Exact juvenile age, iris, flight ability, bill colour, sex ratio, quantified dimorphism, nest form and continuous molt sequence remain unknown. Science status remains false.

Links: [species card](../knowledge/species/141-otus-magicus.md), [run](../production/runs/2026-10-27T1335+0800-cycle-3.md), [v1 archive](https://github.com/janeytlin-eng/game-owl-ket/blob/6b61d3c9ac54f2c9dcb2ccc859904ea66a03df97/owl-pet/design/concepts/141-otus-magicus-v1.png).

**Rejected/unverified lead:** Deshler's 2012 Northern Pygmy-Owl breeding study was found, but the institutional host returned 403 and the Oregon population belongs to a broader historical taxonomic usage. It is not counted as a verified source and supplies no v1 stage detail.

**Design synthesis:** use a central-Mexico high-elevation pineâ€“oak setting, near-equal adults with a subtly fuller/browner female, and an age-unknown, fully supported mesoptile juvenile with unspotted gray crown. Do not combine ORI's undated spotted-head/dark-bill young with Mikkola's mesoptile. Exact age, iris, flight, continuous feather sequence and cavity geometry remain unknown; `scientifically_verified=false`.

## 2026-10-26 #44 Strix uralensis v2 evidence increment

**Verified-source delta:** new 0; reused and freshly read 3 (`UROW-MIKKOLA-2013`, `UROW-LOHMUS-2003`, `UROW-BROMMER-2004`); corrected 0.

Mikkola printed pp.350â€“352 remain the direct adult/young visual boundary: dark-brown adult eyes, yellow-horn bill, long wedge tail with broad pale bars, strongly longitudinally streaked pale underparts, and a roughly four-week young with a developed facial disc but gray-beige fluffy mesoptile. LÃµhmus supports cavities and stick nests with a preference for cavities; Brommer supports a studied nestling period but supplies no drawable feather sequence. Active search also located Eriksson, Lundberg & Westman (1984), *Estimating age of Ural Owl nestlings from body part measurements*, plus a 1987 symposium development figure; direct full-text/figure retrieval failed in this run, so both remain explicitly unverified leads and were not assigned stable source IDs or used for new fine-feather claims. V2 therefore corrects the visible juvenile wing to short soft mesoptile without asserting exact remige geometry or a measured sex-height ratio; `scientifically_verified=false`. | Stable ID | Title / original URL | Author / institution | Year / accessed | Type | Taxon / stage | Verified evidence | Limits / design decision | Links |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SPOT-AVIBASE-2026 | [Athene brama (Spotted Owlet)](https://avibase.bsc-eoc.org/species.jsp?avibaseid=DDE6A35290A28441) | Avibase / Denis Lepage; Clements range source | page current; accessed 2026-10-26 | Institutional taxonomic crosswalk; classification, range and checklist sections read | *Athene brama* species and nominate *A. b. brama* | Confirms current species name, protonym *Strix brama*, nominate range in southern India, open lowland/foothill forest, desert/semi-desert and village habitats; current Avibase, AviList 2025, IOC 15.2 and Clements 2025 retain the species concept. | Taxonomic/range source only; no plumage-development or sex-size evidence. Supports the nominate southern-India setting and current name, not feather geometry. | [species #42](../knowledge/species/42-athene-brama.md); [v2 run](../production/runs/2026-10-26T1550+0800-cycle-6.md) |

**Reuse / uncertainty boundary.** Re-read the complete open primary study `SPOT-PANDE-2011`: eight known-age nestlings from seven intensively studied southern-Indian nests were measured from hatching through 32 days; sexes could not be separated externally; wing growth accelerated after two weeks, reached about 80% of adult chord at 4.5 weeks, and fledging occurred around 4.5 weeks. The authors also observed pre-flight young walking from the nest hole onto branches. For v2, an approximately three-week nestling therefore has a short developing wing, is not shown flying, and remains securely supported at the cavity lip. The paper does not supply a literal feather map; exact remige layering, sex-specific growth and subspecies color variation remain unknown, so `scientifically_verified=false`.

| WESO-ADW-HATCH-2026 | [Otus kennicottii (western screech owl)](https://animaldiversity.org/accounts/Otus_kennicottii/) | Animal Diversity Web, University of Michigan Museum of Zoology | page current; accessed 2026-10-26 | Institutional species account citing Cannings & Angell 2001, Cornell 1999, Ehrlich et al. 1988 | *Megascops kennicottii* (syn. *Otus kennicottii*), hatchling | Altricial young hatch with white down, closed eyes and an egg tooth; male provisions female until young are about three weeks old. | Reproduction / Development text | Secondary synthesis rather than direct day-by-day primary measurements. Reused:
- WESO-CORNELL-ID-2026: adult yellow eyes, deep bill, small ear tufts, compact square-headed silhouette, bark-like streaking and regional color variation.
- WESO-USFS-NM-2010: adult mass dimorphism in sampled New Mexico populations; mass difference is not converted to standing-height difference.
- Cornell Western Screech-Owl life-history page (accessed 2026-10-26): natural/reused cavities, no constructed nest, eggs laid on material already present; cavity dimensions are descriptive, not a universal template.

Design inference: use near-equal adult displayed height with the female only subtly fuller; select a gray-brown shared morph rather than encoding regional color as sex; depict one closed-eye white-down hatchling low on the natural cavity floor with short down-covered wing buds, concealed tail and full body support. These are evidence-bounded visual decisions, not scientific validation.

- DOI/URL: https://doi.org/10.2173/bow.yupowl1.01
- Accessed: 2026-10-12
- Type/stage: institutional synthesis; adult/ecology
- Value: approximately 16 cm; brown/rufous/rare gray morphs; pale dorsal spots, streaked pale underparts, pale tail bands; humid montane/cloud-forest habitat; breeding biology almost unknown.
- Limit/conflict: account access was through indexed text/citation; no juvenile plate or primary developmental series was available. Old woodpecker-hole nesting is an inference.

## 2026-10-13 Â· #40 *Glaucidium nubicola*

### SRC-BOOK-040-01
- Title/author/year: *Owls of the World: A Photographic Guide*, Heimo Mikkola, 2013.
- Pages: printed p.378; uploaded PDF page 380, species 181.
- Type/stages: photographic guide; adult morphology, sex direction, habitat; juvenile status.
- Value: 16 cm, 73â€“80 g, wing 90â€“96 mm; female wings slightly longer but females are not heavier; round head, no ear tufts, yellow eyes, greenish-yellow bill; practically unspotted warm dark-brown mantle/back, bold pale scapular/upperwing-covert spots, short tail with five incomplete pale-spotted bars, white central throat/breast/belly with rufous-streaked flanks; humid western-Andean cloud forest at 1,400â€“2,000 m. The guide explicitly states â€œJuvenile Undescribed.â€
- Limitation: no juvenile morphology or day-age; page image rendering returned 403 in this run, so only extracted page text was used. No invented juvenile plumage is supported.
- Supports: adult identification, near-equal adult scale, habitat, and explicit juvenile uncertainty for #40 v1.

### SRC-PAPER-040-02
- Title/authors/year: *A new species of pygmy-owl (Strigidae: Glaucidium) from the Pacific slope of the northern Andes*, Mark B. Robbins & F. Gary Stiles, 1999, The Auk 116:305â€“315.
- URL: https://www.biologicaldiversity.org/campaigns/global_owl_project/descriptions/Glaucidium/Glaucidium%20nubicola%20%28Robbins%20%26%20Stiles%29%201999%2C%20Auk%2C%20116%2C%20pages%20305-315%20%28front%20cover%29..pdf
- Accessed: 2026-10-13.
- Type/stages: original peer-reviewed species description; adult specimens, voice and range.
- Value: diagnoses the Pacific-slope taxon as distinct and anchors the accepted name, holotype locality/elevation and adult diagnostic comparison.
- Limitation: not a developmental series and does not support juvenile feather chronology.
- Supports: taxonomy and adult diagnostic boundary.

### SRC-PAPER-040-03
- Title/authors/year: *Notes on the breeding of north-west Ecuadorian birds*, Harold F. Greeney & Tony Nunnery, 2006, Bulletin of the British Ornithologistsâ€™ Club 126:38â€“45.
- Accessed: 2026-10-13 through indexed publication citation and breeding excerpt; the primary-paper landing/full-text was not independently rendered in this run.
- Type/stage: original field breeding note; recently fledged juvenile.
- Value: records a recently fledged *G. nubicola* at Tandayapa in August 1999, supporting a mobile, branch-supported fledgling stage and a breeding period extending at least into August.
- Limitation: no directly extracted juvenile plate, exact age, sex, or feather-by-feather description; treated as a stage/behavior boundary only, not a morphology source.
- Supports: use of a conservative, fully supported fledgling pose; does not justify novel plumage detail.

### SRC-WEB-040-04
- Institution/title: BirdLife International, Cloud-forest Pygmy-owl species factsheet.
- URL: https://datazone.birdlife.org/species/factsheet/cloud-forest-pygmy-owl-glaucidium-nubicola
- Accessed: 2026-10-13.
- Type: institutional taxonomy, range and conservation account.
- Value: accepted name and Vulnerable conservation context.
- Limitation: dynamic page did not expose juvenile morphology or sex-specific measurements in this reading.
- Supports: current-name/range/status cross-check only.


## 2026-10-13 Â· #41 *Glaucidium cobanense*

### SRC-BOOK-041-01
- Title/author/year: *Owls of the World: A Photographic Guide*, Heimo Mikkola, 2013.
- Pages: printed p.380; uploaded PDF p.382, species 183.
- Type/stages: photographic guide; adult, downy chick and mesoptile.
- Value: 16â€“18 cm, wing 82â€“98 mm; no sexual-size data; brown and red morphs, red predominant; yellow eyes, yellowish-horn bill, no ear tufts, false eyes and barred tail. Downy chick whitish; mesoptile adult-like but more diffuse, with grayish unmarked crown and mottled forehead. Mountain forest at higher elevations.
- Limitation: no exact juvenile age or continuous wing/tail development; no sex-size evidence.
- Supports: #41 adult/mesoptile boundaries and explicit near-equal sex scale.

### SRC-PAPER-041-02
- Title/authors/year: *Vocalizations of the Guatemalan Pygmy-Owl (Glaucidium cobanense)*, Knut Eisermann & Steve N. G. Howell, 2011, Journal of Raptor Research 45(4).
- DOI/URL: https://doi.org/10.3356/JRR-10-73.1
- Accessed: 2026-10-13.
- Type: peer-reviewed taxonomic/vocal study.
- Value: documents vocal divergence and the highland distribution used by modern species-level treatments.
- Limitation/conflict: AOS/Clements treatments have historically retained it within *G. gnoma*; this source supports an evidence-based split but does not erase checklist disagreement. No juvenile morphology.
- Supports: current working species boundary and old-name mapping *G. gnoma cobanense*.

### SRC-WEB-041-03
- Institution/authors: CAYAYA Birding / Knut Eisermann & Claudia AvendaÃ±o, Natural history of Guatemalan Pygmy-Owl.
- URL: https://www.bird-studies-guatemala.info/Guatemalan-Pygmy-Owl.htm
- Accessed: 2026-10-13.
- Type/stage: field-research project page; breeding/nest.
- Value: researchers report first Guatemalan nesting records and ongoing breeding-biology work; associated video index documents juveniles at a nest entrance.
- Limitation: results were stated as being prepared for publication; no quantitative day-age or feather sequence was extracted. Used only to support tree-cavity/nest-entrance context, not precise plumage.

### SRC-WEB-041-04
- Institution/title: Avibase taxonomic concepts, Guatemalan Pygmy-Owl.

- Type: checklist aggregation/taxonomic crosswalk.
- Value: maps *Glaucidium cobanense* to historical/alternate *Glaucidium gnoma cobanense* usage.
- Limitation: taxonomy only; no morphology or development.

### OWL-SRC-0049-A
- Title: African Wood Owl
- Author/institution: SANBI / Rural Citizen Science Birding Project
- Year: 2018
- URL: https://www.sanbi.org/animal-of-the-week/african-wood-owl/
- Accessed: 2026-10-13
- Type: institutional species account
- Applies to: *Strix woodfordii*; adult ecology, breeding and nest site
- Evidence read: dense evergreen/deciduous/riparian woodland; natural tree-hole nesting; references Steyn & Scott breeding work.
- Location: habitat and life-cycle sections; lines 33â€“45, 77â€“102 in retrieved page.
- Limit: synthesized account; not a continuous juvenile plumage series.
- Design support: dense woodland and cavity setting; no exact subspecies claim.
- Confidence: medium-high.

### OWL-SRC-0049-B
- Title: Owls of Levubu / African Wood Owl observations
- Author/institution: African Birdlife / BirdLife South Africa
- Year: 2016
- URL: https://www.birdlife.org.za/wp-content/uploads/2020/08/38-47_Owls4.pdf
- Accessed: 2026-10-13
- Type: field-observation article with nest photographs
- Applies to: *Strix woodfordii*; chicks, nest and branching stage
- Evidence read: PDF pp.46â€“47 (file page 4), lines 283â€“434; nests 7â€“15 m high in palm sheaths/hollow logs; female leaves nest about 10 days after hatch; chicks exercise another 2â€“3 weeks and climb surrounding branches at 25â€“30 days; caption identifies almost-one-month chick clambering around nest.
- Limit: locality-specific Levubu observations; plumage sequence not quantified.
- Design support: supported 25â€“30-day branching juvenile at hollow-log rim, not a flying chick.
- Confidence: high for behavior/stage, medium for generalized plumage.

### OWL-SRC-0049-C
- Title: African Wood-owl
- Author/institution: The Peregrine Fund
- Year: undated current account
- URL: https://peregrinefund.org/explore-raptors-species/owls/african-wood-owl
- Accessed: 2026-10-13
- Type: institutional species account; cites Birds of the World v1.1
- Applies to: *Strix woodfordii*; hatchling through fledging and habitat
- Evidence read: lines 40â€“64; white-down hatchling, natural cavities, branching around one month, first flight around 46 days; cites DOI 10.2173/bow.afwowl1.01.1.
- Limit: summary account rather than full primary paper.
- Design support: white/pale branching juvenile with short developing wings; no flight pose at 25â€“30 days.
- Confidence: medium-high.

### OWL-SRC-0049-D
- Title: African Wood Owl â€“ *Strix woodfordii*

- Year: current page

## #53 *Strix nebulosa*

- Title: *Strix nebulosa*, Great Gray Owl

- Accessed: 2026-10-13
- Type: institutional literature synthesis
- Species/stage: adult, nestling, recently fledged juvenile
- Useful facts: young leave nests at 26â€“28.5 days; remain unable to fly for 7â€“14 days; climb leaning/deeply fissured trees with talons, wings and bill; documents nest sites, habitat and post-fledging support needs.
- Section: Development and Reproduction; Perching and Roosting; Nesting Habitat
- Limitations/confidence: high for cited developmental ranges and behavior; synthesized from multiple studies rather than a new primary observation.
- Design decision: preflight juvenile shown low and supported while climbing leaning bark, with no mature flight posture.

### OWL-SRC-0053-B

- Authors/institution: Evelyn L. Bull and Mark G. Henjum; USDA Forest Service Pacific Northwest Research Station
- Year: 1990
- URL/DOI: https://research.fs.usda.gov/treesearch/5628 ; https://doi.org/10.2737/PNW-GTR-265
- Accessed: 2026-10-13
- Type: primary field study / General Technical Report
- Species/stage: 24 pairs, 107 juveniles, 71 nesting attempts
- Useful facts: nests occurred on stick platforms, broken-off dead-tree tops and artificial platforms; mature/older mixed conifer stands common; recently fledged young need perches and cover.

- Limitations/confidence: high for northeastern Oregon population; regional extrapolation is limited.
- Design decision: broken snag/old platform context and nearby leaning-tree support, not a cavity nest.

### OWL-SRC-0053-C

- Design decision: supports down-dominant, recently nest-departed stage and non-cavity nesting structure.

## 2026-10-14 Â· #55 *Bubo lacteus* (current combination *Ketupa lactea*)

### VEO-PF-2026
- Title: Verreaux's Eagle-owl
- Institution: The Peregrine Fund
- URL: https://peregrinefund.org/explore-raptors-species/owls/verreauxs-eagle-owl
- Accessed: 2026-10-14
- Type: raptor-conservation institution species account; adult, nestling and nest-leaving juvenile
- Evidence actually read: lines 17â€“22, 42â€“50 and 68â€“77. Pink eyelids can appear about one week after hatch; adults have dark eyes, creamy dark-edged facial disc, small ear tufts and fine pale vermiculation. Large reused nests and occasional hollows are documented. At about 60 days, young leave the nest before reliable flight, climbing and hopping in the nest tree; first flight follows about two weeks later.
- Limits/confidence: institutional synthesis rather than a primary growth-series paper; age values support behavior, not an exact plumage chronology. High for stage/action and diagnostics, medium for fine juvenile feather detail.
- Design support: branch-supported, non-flying ~60-day climber with down-softened outline and short developing wings; diagnostic pink eyelid allowed without adult feather maturity.

### VEO-CZBG-2026
- Title: Verreaux's Eagle Owl
- Institution: Cincinnati Zoo & Botanical Garden
- URL: https://cincinnatizoo.org/animals-archive/verreauxs-eagle-owl/
- Accessed: 2026-10-14
- Type: accredited zoo species account
- Evidence actually read: lines 66â€“70 and 77â€“90. Identifies *Bubo lacteus*, Africa's largest owl, central-to-southern African range, acacia/woodland/savanna habitat; usually one chick reaches fledging and young may remain with the pair.
- Limits/confidence: no sex-specific measurements or feather-sequence data. High for identity/habitat, medium for generalized family behavior.
- Design support: dry wooded-savanna setting and restrained family context only.

### VEO-TAX-2026
- Title: Verreaux's eagle-owl current taxonomic treatment
- Institution: current secondary synthesis cross-referencing modern owl-systematics sources
- URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Verreaux%27s_eagle-owl
- Accessed: 2026-10-14
- Type: taxonomic routing source
- Evidence actually read: lines 148â€“181. Current combination shown as *Ketupa lactea*; historical/project combination *Bubo lacteus* retained as synonym mapping.
- Limits/confidence: secondary source; used only to prevent duplicate counting across genus changes, not for morphology or juvenile design.
- Design support: one species record, not two; plate follows repository label *Bubo lacteus*.

- Evidence actually read: PDF p.1 lines 17â€“35 and p.2 lines 51â€“60. Orange eyes, prominent ear tufts, tawny mottled brown upperparts, buff lowerparts with black streaks and a whitish throat; males are smaller than females. Rocky scrapes/crevices/cave entrances/sheltered cliff ledges are used. Young begin only short conditioning flights at about 60 days.

- Title: Spot-bellied Eagle Owl *Bubo nipalensis* in northern Eastern Ghats, Andhra Pradesh, India
- Author/source: Sathyanarayana Srinivasan; Indian BIRDS 8(4):106â€“107
- URL: https://indianbirds.in/pdfs/IB_8.4_Final.pdf
- Accessed: 2026-10-14
- Type: field/rescue observation with dated juvenile photographs
- Applies to: juvenile plumage, movement ability, moist-forest setting
- Evidence actually read: PDF pp.106â€“107 (file pp.24â€“25), lines 1981â€“2074. Juvenile was pale yellow with light spotting, thin V-shaped breast marks, heavily barred dark flight/tail feathers, small ear tufts and creamy-yellow legs; it attempted escape but did not fly far and was judged not yet flying/dependent. Dense moist deciduous forest with semi-evergreen/evergreen stream pockets is documented.
- Limits/confidence: rescued single individual of unknown exact age; high for visible plumage/observed weak flight, not a continuous growth series.
- Design support: supported branch-perched dependent juvenile with pale yellow-cream plumage, small ear tufts and restrained wing presentation; no strong flight pose.

### SBEO-OWL-PAGES-2022
- Title: Forest Eagle Owl (*Bubo nipalensis*) juvenile
- Author/source: Anawat Jorapunyanont / The Owl Pages
- Date/location: March 2022, Kaeng Krachan National Park, Thailand
- URL: https://www.owlpages.com/owls/species.php?i=1120
- Accessed: 2026-10-14
- Type: specialist labelled field photograph
- Evidence actually read: page lines 49â€“54 identify a young bird perched on a branch in daylight.
- Limits/confidence: no exact age or measurements; medium-high for supported perching stage.
- Design support: stable branch support and daylight forest context only.

### SBEO-THAI-NP-2026
- Title: Spot-bellied eagle-owl â€” *Bubo nipalensis*
- Institution: Thai National Parks species account
- URL: https://www.thainationalparks.com/species/spot-bellied-eagle-owl
- Accessed: 2026-10-14
- Type: regional species synthesis
- Evidence actually read: lines 24â€“38 and 43â€“50. Adults are large stark grayish-brown forest owls with very long ragged sideways ear tufts, pale fulvous underparts carrying black-and-white horizontal flank stripes and broad abdominal spots; feathered legs/feet with bare terminal digits. Juveniles are much paler grayish-cream with heavy brownish barring. Primary/older secondary moist forests are typical.
- Limits/confidence: synthesis rather than primary morphology paper; does not establish a stable visible sex difference.
- Design support: adult diagnostic pattern, juvenile colour boundary and dense forest setting; adults kept near-equal in visible scale.

### SBEO-TAX-2026
- Title: Spot-bellied eagle-owl taxonomic routing
- Source: current synthesis / historical literature mapping
- URL: https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spot-bellied_eagle-owl
- Accessed: 2026-10-14
- Type: taxonomy routing source
- Evidence actually read: current combination presented as *Ketupa nipalensis* while extensive recent literature and project label use *Bubo nipalensis*.
- Limits/confidence: secondary source; taxonomy mapping only.
- Design support: one species record under historical/current genus combinations; no duplicate credit.

## Uploaded Mikkola2013 document reuse and historical correction Â· 2026-10-14
Existing WHSO-MIKKOLA-2013 identifies the same uploaded 2nd-edition book; use that document identity rather than duplicating it as six new books. This run successfully read text/captions, not photograph pixels, on these printed pages (PDF pagination +2):
- Tyto soumagnei pp98â€“99: no sex-size data; reddish-ochre, smoky toes; relatively long tail conflicts with ADW short-tail wording. #65 chosen older stage retained, precise tail length calibration pending.
- Tyto capensis pp104â€“105: dull yellow/light golden second coat, mainly brown juvenile face/white edges; male wing/tail may be longer, sexed weights absent. Conflicts with broad institution female-larger statement by metric, not resolved; #66 display scale not a measured ratio, mark pending primary study. DOI10.2989/OSTRICH.2008.79.1.11.366 retrieval failed; no primary measurement claimed.
- Pulsatrix perspicillata pp310â€“311: grey-brown barred mesoptile wing-coverts, brown barred wing/tail; cycle3 all-white wings inappropriate inference, v2 correction in cycle5. Eastern Costa Rica unbarred-belly chapmani visual direction distinguished from western finely barred saturata.
- Tyto tenebricosa pp114â€“115: usually female heavier but reversed pair reported; whitish/grey-white natal down conflicts with OwlPages sooty-grey wording, avoided by #68 NSW-supported older fledgling. Australian nominate selected, not New Guinea arfaki.
- Tyto novaehollandiae p107: female longer wings; creamy second down generalization, but Debus2007 dark-morph individual buff coat will take priority. Museum white-to-cream fledgling description not applied to all morphs. Tasmanian castanops separate old concept retained, not counted as extra species here.
- Ninox strenua pp450â€“451: male larger; mesoptile white face/dark eye patches, white sparsely streaked underside, barred wings. Caption young wings/tail may resemble adult, so cannot mechanically shorten all to wing buds. Candidate research only, no production credit yet.

Historical run records saying guide not read remain true for their earlier stage; this later access adds evidence and triggers targeted review, not retroactive scientific approval. Updated mappings link [65](../knowledge/species/65-tyto-soumagnei.md), [66](../knowledge/species/66-tyto-capensis.md), [67v2](../design/concepts/67-pulsatrix-perspicillata-v2.png), [68](../knowledge/species/68-tyto-tenebricosa.md), planned69 and research-only Ninox strenua. ## #87 Bubo ascalaphus
#87 Bubo ascalaphus â€” Pharaoh Eagle-Owl
Accessed2026-10-15.
ASC-MIK2013-272: Heimo Mikkola, Owls of the World2013, uploaded book printed272â€“273/PDF274â€“275 text actually read. Sandy tawny/rufous upperparts dark mottling, rounded tawny face finely dark-rimmed, yellow to deeporange iris, blackbill, feathered tarsi/toes, shorter pointed tufts than B.bubo; upperbreast dark drop shaftstreaks, fine abdominal markings. Female averages400g heavier (not a height ratio). Whitish down with dull yellow, fluffy mesoptile barred above, slight upperbreast barring/weak tufts. Book monotypic; historical desertorum pale regional variation noted, no new taxon/count inferred. Photo caption juvenile EgyptFebruary notpixelsinspected.
ASC-ALMASAEED2024: Matar Al-Masaeed, Omar Al-Hazzaâ€™a, Nader Al-Ghiath, Tareq Qaneer,Nashat Hamidan/RSCN, Breeding Record of Pharaoh Eagle-Owl (Bubo ascalaphus) in the Eastern Desert of Jordan, Jordan Journal of Natural History11(2),2024 pp142â€“143 (contents141 disagrees witharticleheading). https://www.rscn.org.jo/uploaded_files/journal/679098e3881271737529571.pdf fullPDF downloaded/textread; printed142/PDF72 actually rendered/viewed. Nest at margin gully/runoff EasternRisha,3eggs15March2024; Figure1 shows ground scrape beside shrub/rock not woven nest; earlyApril3chickshatched, flashfloodkilledall. Does NOT establish juvenile appearance or flightability. Primary wild nest record.
ASC-HAJWAL2015-953: Faisal Hajwal, Pharaoh Eagle Owl juvenile with prey, SaudiArabiaApril2015; original photo and caption actually read/viewed https://www.owlpages.com/owls/species.php?i=953 . Fuzzy ochre/tawny juvenile, yelloweyes darkbill, faint bars, weak/no tufts; gripping rock and holding small lizard. Exactage,sex,flightability unknown; one photograph not developmental series. No reproduction of photo inrepo.
Design inference: similarsexplumage, female modestlyfuller notfixedheight ratio; middle fuzzy mesoptilejuvenile onbroadrock gripping naturally, noage/flightclaim, tinybeakopen upwardsolicitation withoutprey copying. Desertwadiusable notcavitytree/wovennest. Preserve uncertainty in exactfeathersequence/toes/sexratio; sciencefalse. Newexternal2, existingbookreuse1 newsectionmapping1. Sourceindex map Buboascalaphusâ†’ASC IDs; historical B.buboascalaphus same species no double count.

### BRA-MOTTA-BRAGA2012 â€” literature-gap screening (not juvenile morphology proof)
- JosÃ© Carlos Motta-Junior & Ana ClÃ¡udia Rocha Braga (2012), Estado del conocimiento sobre la ecologÃ­a y biologÃ­a de bÃºhos en Brasil. Ornitologia Neotropical23:227â€“234. OriginalURL https://sora.unm.edu/sites/default/files/Motta-Junior.pdf ; accessed2026-10-16; literature review, actual fulltext read (pp227,228â€“229 methods,232 table1,233 discussion).
- Scope Brazilian owls incl. Megascops sanctaecatarinae/M. atricapilla, mixed taxa and research topics, not age-specific primary development. Authors reviewed262 works through firsthalf2011, many partial observations; morphology/age cannot be reconstructed from numberofreferences.
- Designdecision: do NOT use review table as evidence for candidate juvenile feathering or fledging days; follow original species-specific nest papers. Older nomenclature permethods/KonigWeick2008, not current taxonomy authority. Confidence high for paper's own reviewmethods, insufficient for individual young appearance. No imageversion produced fromthissource.
- Linkedrun [remaining cycle4](../production/runs/2026-10-16T0146+0800-cycle-4.md); taxa map Megascops sanctaecatarinae / Megascops atricapilla; topic evidencegaps/reproductionliterature.


## 2026-10-16 cycle4 incremental screening â€” no image/cycle credit

### ABY-OGADA-MURIITHI2017
- Darcy Ogada (The Peregrine Fund), Paul Muriithi (Kenya Wildlife Service Training Institute), June2017, Is the Abyssinian Owl on Mt Kenya dependent on declining heath forest habitat? FinalProjectReport toAfricanBirdClub. https://www.africanbirdclub.org/sites/default/files/2016_Abyssinian_Owl.pdf ; access2026-10-16. Original fieldresearch report, actual nine-page text read; summaryp2/resultsp4/conclusionsp6 (PDF pages2/4/6).
- Taxon Asio abyssinicus, MtKenya; no juvenile developmental sequence. Authors explicitly didnot positivelyconfirm owl identity despite82 fielddays and30 cameratrappingmonths. Roost/pellets suspected only; habitat dependence preliminary.
- Decision: do not use pellet prey/roost vegetation as confirmed species-specific nest or juvenile evidence; do not infer absence/extinction from nondetection. Reliable evidenceof ownmethod/limitations, not positive juvenile morphology. No subspecies/youngage inference; no renderedversion.

### LTS-PAGANO2021
- LuisG.Pagano, AlejandroBodrati, NestorFariÃ±a, FacundoG.DiSallo, MarceloJ.Wioneczak, LuisS.Pradier, ErnestoR.Krauczuk, LuisO.Krause, KristinaL.Cockle (2021-12-01), DistribuciÃ³n e historia natural del AlilicucÃº Orejudo o RibereÃ±o (Megascops sanctaecatarinae) en Argentina. ElHornero36(2):51â€“64. DOI10.56178/eh.v36i2.370 ; https://elhornero.avesargentinas.org.ar/home/article/view/370 ; access2026-10-16. Originalobservationpaper, actual journalHTML author/abstract/reference sections read; PDFdownload toolinternalerror, fullPDF NOTread.
- Taxon M.sanctaecatarinae, MisionesArgentina; abstract41records/18localities; vocalizations especiallyfemale important; bodylargeness/scaledchest canhelp but notalwaysvisible; tuftlength/iriscolour notdiagnostic alone. Study reportedriparian/degraded/recoveringforest association, not universal specieshabitat restriction.
- Decision: avoid treating longtufts/yellowvsorangeeyes as sufficient speciesverification; full developmental evidence notpresent inreadabstract. ### PSC-KOMAR-STUFKENS-2012
- Oliver Komar (2012), â€œReflexiones sobre mi primer aÃ±o observando aves en el campus de la Escuela AgrÃ­cola Panamericana (Universidad Zamorano), Valle del Yeguareâ€, El Esmeralda 1(2), pp59â€“66; Fig4 p65 photo Paul Stufkens, 27May2012. Institution-hosted original: https://www.aveshonduras.org/wp-content/uploads/2021/11/El-Esmeralda_Vol.-1-No.-2_2012.pdf ; accessed2026-10-16. Primary field observation/photo, Megascops cooperi (old Otus cooperi), Zamorano Honduras, fledgling, exact age/sex unknown. Actual PDF downloaded, relevant text pp59â€“60,65â€“66 read, p65 rendered and visually inspected. Figure shows gray soft-headed, finely barred youngster spreading developed barred wings in defense, feet supported on ground; not a flying neonate. Text describes two adults and one fledgling initially in tree; after thrush disturbance youngster fell and had difficulty flying, observer returned it near parents. This is one event, not a universal flight limit or developmental age curve. P60 campus740â€“815m contradicts treating330m as universal upper elevation. Use ground-supported defensive pose; natural root/leaf setting is design inference, not copied photo location. Sex-size ratio remains unknown. Photo/PDF copyrighted CC BY-NC-ND3.0; no PDF/figure redistributed. Confidence high for this reported event, limited generalization. Related planned #118, [cycle1](../production/runs/2026-10-16T1747+0800-cycle-1.md).

### PSC-OWLPAGES-2020
- Deane Lewis, Pacific Screech Owlâ€”Megascops cooperi, OwlPages, 2020-10-23, https://www.owlpages.com/owls/species.php?s=870 ; accessed2026-10-16. Secondary species account, actual Description/Habitat/Breeding read. Adult pale gray facial disc, yellow irises, greenish bill/cere, prominent barred ear tufts, gray-brown cryptic upperparts; thin shafts and fine vermiculation below. Tarsi feathered to toe bases, fleshy toes with bristles. Supports adult color/feather boundary, not quantified sexual dimorphism or juvenile age.
